) we conclude that the first two components of the current are explicitly given by

(4.39)

\displaystyle j_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{R}^{2}_{x}-\text{id})\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{1}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}+\mathsf{S}_{2}-\mathsf{H}_{2}+\left(\text{id}-\mathcal{R}_{x}^{2}\right)\textsf{G}_{2}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\textsf{G}_{1}-\frac{J_{1}}{\pi L^{2}},

(4.40)

\displaystyle j_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{R}^{2}_{y}-\text{id}\right)\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j^{1}_{0}-\mathsf{S}_{1}+\mathsf{H}_{1}-\left(\text{id}-\mathcal{R}_{y}^{2}\right)\textsf{G}_{1}-\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\textsf{G}_{2}+\frac{J_{2}}{\pi L^{2}}.

To conclude the formal derivation of the integral equation
j_{0}
we have to determine the values of the constants
J_{1},J_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. As in the linearized case, the values
J_{1},J_{2}
are crucial to ensure that the constructed pressure
p
is a uni-valued function. We take the constants to be

(4.41)

\displaystyle\frac{J_{2}}{\pi L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\left\langle\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}\right\rangle+\left\langle\textsf{A}(\delta j^{2})\right\rangle+\langle\textsf{G}_{1}\rangle-\langle j_{0}^{2}f\rangle+\langle g_{2}(\partial g_{2}-\partial_{2}g_{1})\rangle.

(4.42)

\displaystyle\frac{J_{1}}{\pi L^{2}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\left\langle\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}\right\rangle-\left\langle\textsf{A}(\delta j^{2})\right\rangle+\langle\textsf{G}_{1}\rangle-\langle j_{0}^{2}f\rangle+\langle g_{2}(\partial g_{2}-\partial_{2}g_{1})\rangle.

The reason of this choice will be clarified in Section
6
. All in all, we conclude that the system of integral equations we want to solve is formally given by the expression

\displaystyle j_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{R}^{2}_{x}-\text{id})\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\widetilde{\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{1}}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}+\mathsf{S}_{2}[\widetilde{\delta j^{1}},\delta j^{2}]-\mathsf{H}_{2}+\left(\text{id}-\mathcal{R}_{x}^{2}\right)\widetilde{\textsf{G}_{2}}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\textsf{G}_{1}

(4.43)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\langle(\partial_{1}g_{2}-\partial_{2}g_{1})g_{1}\rangle-\langle j^{1}_{0}f\rangle,

\displaystyle j_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{R}^{2}_{y}-\text{id}\right)\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\widetilde{\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{2}}+\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\sum_{\kappa=1}^{4}\textsf{T}_{\kappa}j^{1}_{0}-\mathsf{S}_{1}[\delta j^{1},\widetilde{\delta j^{2}}]+\mathsf{H}_{1}-\left(\text{id}-\mathcal{R}_{y}^{2}\right)\widetilde{\textsf{G}_{1}}-\mathcal{R}_{x}\mathcal{R}_{y}\textsf{G}_{2}

(4.44)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\langle(\partial_{1}g_{2}-\partial_{2}g_{1})g_{2}\rangle-\langle j^{2}_{0}f\rangle.

We recall that the overline notation
\widetilde{v}
is defined as
\widetilde{v}=v-\langle v\rangle
.

5.
Rigorous derivation of integral current equation and a priori estimates

In this section, we provide a rigorous approach to the formal computations introduced in Section
4
. More precisely, we use the pseudo-differential approach in Hölder spaces (mainly contained in Subsection
2.1
-Subsection
2.3
) to show that the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa}
in (
4.39
)-(
4.40
) are well defined bounded operators in Hölder spaces, cf. Theorem
5.9
. We will also show estimates for the differences of those operators in lower order norms, cf. Proposition
5.13
. Furthermore, we will also provide several estimates for the remaining operators
\mathsf{S}_{\ell},\mathsf{H}_{\ell}
and
\mathsf{G}_{\ell}
for
\ell=1,2,
which will be needed in Section
6
to solve the integral equation (
4.43
)-(
4.44
) via Neumann series. These bounds are contained in Proposition
5.17
, Proposition
5.16
and Corollary
5.15
, respectively.

5.1.
Hölder estimates for the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa}

Let us start this subsection by showing that the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa}
are well-defined operators and that
\mathsf{T}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{L}(C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
. To that purpose, we recall their precise expression:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{1}j_{0}^{\ell}(r)

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}e^{i(r-\eta)\cdot\xi}(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(\eta,s)}-1}{1+\Theta(\eta,s)}\right)ds\right)j_{0}^{\ell}(\eta)d\eta,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{2}j_{0}^{\ell}(r)

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}e^{i(r-\eta)\cdot\xi}(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{\Theta(\eta,s)}{1+\Theta(\eta,s)}\right)ds\right)j_{0}^{\ell}(\eta)d\eta,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{3}j_{0}^{\ell}(r)

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}e^{i(r-\eta)\cdot\xi}(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)\left(\frac{e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(\eta,s)}-1}{1+\Theta(\eta,s)}\right)ds\right)j_{0}^{\ell}(\eta)d\eta,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{4}j_{0}^{\ell}(r)

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}e^{i(r-\eta)\cdot\xi}(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\left(\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)\left(\frac{\Theta(\eta,s)}{1+\Theta(\eta,s)}\right)ds\right)j_{0}^{\ell}(\eta)d\eta,

for
\ell=1,2,
and where we recall that
(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}=\frac{|\xi|\sinh(|\xi|L)}{\cosh(|\xi|L)-1}
. In order show that the operators
\mathsf{T}_{\kappa}
for
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
are well-defined, the main idea is to define them as the adjoint of a pseudo-differential operator with associated symbols

(5.5)

\displaystyle a_{1}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\left(e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds,

(5.6)

\displaystyle a_{2}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\Theta(x,s)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds,

(5.7)

\displaystyle a_{3}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\left(e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds,

(5.8)

\displaystyle a_{4}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\Theta(x,s)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds.

Indeed, notice that formally

\langle\phi,\mathsf{T}_{\kappa}j_{0}^{\ell}\rangle=\langle\text{Op}(a)_{\kappa}\phi,j_{0}^{\ell}\rangle,

and hence due to Theorem
2.10
, if we show that the symbols (
5.5
)-(
5.8
) belong to the symbol class
S^{0}(1+\alpha)
the operators
\mathsf{T}_{\kappa}
are well-defined and bounded in the corresponding Hölder spaces, cf. Theorem
2.10
for precise statement. Notice that we are in the situation described in Remark
2.11
, so we just need to prove that the obvious extensions of the symbols
a_{\kappa}(x,\xi)
,
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
, satisfy estimates (
2.13
).

In order to simplify the exposition, we define the symbols

(5.9)

\displaystyle b_{1}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds,

(5.10)

\displaystyle b_{2}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\Theta(x,s)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds.

Therefore, it is easy to check that

a_{1}(x,\xi)=b_{1}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\quad a_{2}(x,\xi)=b_{2}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi),

where
\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi)
are smoothing remainders. Therefore, we will directly work with the new symbols
b_{1}(x,\xi),b_{2}(x,\xi)
and later also show that the same estimates and conclusions trivially hold for the remainder terms
\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi)
, and hence for
a_{1}(x,\xi),a_{2}(x,\xi)
. The fast decay of the function
M(\xi,s)
defined in (
4.21
), provides that the kernels defined out of
a_{3}(x,\xi),a_{4}(x,\xi)
are smooth and as a consequence the operators
\textsf{T}_{3}
and
\textsf{T}_{4}
are smoothing operators. This fact will be shown in Proposition
5.7
.

Furthermore, we consider that the functions
\Lambda
and
\Theta
are generic functions that satisfy the following two requirements:

Assumption 5.1
.

The function
\Lambda:\Omega\to\mathbb{T}^{2}
belongs to
C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega)
and satisfies that
\Lambda(x,0)=0
. Moreover, we have that there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that

\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq\delta_{0}.

Assumption 5.2
.

The function
\Theta:\Omega\to\mathbb{T}^{2}
belongs to
C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega)
and satisfies that

\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq\delta_{1},\mbox{ for }\delta_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}).

We will see later that indeed the functions
\Lambda
and
\Theta
do indeed satisfy these requirements, cf. Proposition
5.19
. In the following result, we show that under Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
, the symbols
(
5.9
)-(
5.10
) extend to symbols in
S^{0}(1+\alpha)
, cf. Definition
2.3
. If so, a direct application of Theorem
2.10
, yields that the associated operators
\mathsf{T}_{1},\mathsf{T}_{2}
are well-defined bounded operators from
C^{2,\alpha}
to
C^{2,\alpha}
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
hold. Then, the symbols
b_{1},b_{2}
given in (
5.9
)-(
5.10
) respectively, extend to symbols in
S^{0}(1+\alpha)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

Let us first demonstrate the result for the symbol
b_{1}(x,\xi)
defined in (
5.9
). To that purpose, recalling that the symbol
\xi
is a Mihklin-Hörmander multiplier of degree 1, we have that

b_{1}\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)\Longleftrightarrow\frac{b_{1}}{|\xi|}\in S^{-1}(1+\alpha).

In order to show that claim, we begin by pointing out that for any
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
multi-index,

(5.11)

\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}e^{-|\xi|s}=e^{-|\xi|s}\sum_{\kappa}s^{k^{\gamma}_{\kappa}}p^{\gamma}_{\kappa}(\xi),

with
k_{\kappa}^{\gamma}\leq|\gamma|
and
p^{\gamma}_{\kappa}(\xi)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\})
satisfying

(5.12)

\lvert\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}p^{\gamma}_{\kappa}(\xi)\rvert\lesssim|\xi|^{k^{\gamma}_{\kappa}-|\gamma|}.

We remark that the summation in (
5.11
) is finite. For simplicity we do not precise where the summation index
\kappa
is taken. Moreover, for
\gamma,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
multi-index, we write
\beta<\gamma
if the
\beta
is less than
\gamma
componentwise.

L^{\infty}
bounds for
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(b_{1}(x,\xi)/|\xi|\right)

Invoking Leibniz rule and recalling definition (
5.9
) we infer that

\displaystyle\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(\frac{b_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\frac{\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds+\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)\frac{ds}{1+\Theta(x,s)}

(5.13)

\displaystyle=b_{11}(x,\xi)+b_{12}(x,\xi).

Taking into account Assumption
5.1
we find that

(5.14)

\left|e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{s}i\xi\cdot\partial_{w}\Lambda(x,w)e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,w)}dw\right|\leq s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}.

Combining (
5.11
)-(
5.14
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|b_{11}(x,\xi)\right|\leq\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\sum_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{L}|\xi|^{1-|\gamma|+k^{\gamma}_{\kappa}}s^{k^{\gamma}_{\kappa}+1}e^{-|\xi|s}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\displaystyle\sum_{\kappa_{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{k^{\gamma}_{\kappa}+1}e^{-u}du

(5.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}.

In order to bound
b_{12}(x,\xi)
, we first notice that

(5.16)

\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)\\
=e^{-|\xi|s}\sum_{\kappa}s^{k^{\beta}_{\kappa}}p^{\beta}_{\kappa}(\xi)\left(i\Lambda(x,s)\right)^{\gamma-\beta}e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}.

Using (
5.16
), the fact that
\beta\neq\gamma
and Assumption
5.1
we find that

\displaystyle\lvert b_{12}(x,\xi)\rvert\leq\frac{\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\sum_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{L}|\xi|^{k_{\kappa}^{\beta}-|\beta|}s^{k^{\beta}_{\kappa}+|\gamma-\beta|}e^{-|\xi|s}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\sum_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{k^{\beta}_{\kappa}+|\gamma-\beta|}e^{-u}du

(5.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}.

Hence, bounds (
5.15
)-(
5.17
) yield the decay

\left|\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(\frac{b_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)\right|\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\frac{\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\bigg)|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1},\ \forall\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\ \mbox{multi-index}.

L^{\infty}
bounds for
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\nabla_{x}\left(b_{1}(x,\xi)/|\xi|\right)

In order to obtain
L^{\infty}
estimates we compute

(5.18)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\left(\frac{b_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\,i\nabla_{x}\Lambda\cdot\xi\frac{e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds-\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)}{(1+\Theta(x,s))^{2}}\nabla_{x}\Theta(x,s)ds.

Combining (
5.18
) with Leibniz rule we infer that

\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\nabla_{x}\left(\frac{b_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)=b_{13}(x,\xi)-b_{14}(x,\xi),

where

\displaystyle b_{13}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)i\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\frac{ds}{1+\Theta(x,s)}

(5.19)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)i\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right)\frac{ds}{1+\Theta(x,s)},

\displaystyle b_{14}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)\frac{\nabla_{x}\Theta(x,s)}{(1+\Theta(x,s))^{2}}\ ds

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)\frac{\nabla_{x}\Theta(x,s)}{(1+\Theta(x,s))^{2}}\ ds.

The term
b_{14}(x,\xi)
has the same structure as (
5.13
) and hence performing the same estimates we find that

(5.21)

\lvert b_{14}(x,\xi)\rvert\lesssim\left(\frac{\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1}}}{\left(1-\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\frac{\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{1}}^{|\gamma-\beta|}\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1}}}{\left(1-\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{2}}\right)\lvert\xi\rvert^{-|\gamma|-1}\leq\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

Using (
5.16
) and the cancellation property in Assumption
5.1
, namely,
\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,0)=0
we can compute similarly that

(5.22)

\lvert b_{13}(x,\xi)\rvert\lesssim\left(\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}^{|\gamma-\beta|}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\right)|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

Therefore, combining (
5.21
)-(
5.22
), we have that

\displaystyle\left|\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\nabla_{x}\left(\frac{b_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\frac{\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}^{|\gamma-\beta|}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\right)|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{1}}\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1}}}{\left(1-\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\frac{\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{1}}^{|\gamma-\beta|}\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1}}}{\left(1-\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{2}}\right)\lvert\xi\rvert^{-|\gamma|-1}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

C^{\alpha}
bounds for
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\nabla_{x}\left(b_{1}(x,\xi)/|\xi|\right)

To finally show that the symbol
b_{1}(x,\xi)\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)
, we have to prove that the
C^{\alpha}
norms for the terms (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) have the desired decay. More precisely, we will obtain the following bounds

(5.24)

\displaystyle\|b_{13}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\alpha|},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\|b_{14}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\alpha|}.

Through the derivation of the bounds (
5.24
)-(
5.25
) we will repeatedly employ the elementary inequality

(5.26)

[fgh]_{\alpha}\leq[f]_{\alpha}\|g\|_{\infty}\|h\|_{\infty}+\|f\|_{\infty}[g]_{\alpha}\|h\|_{\infty}+\|f\|_{\infty}\|g\|_{\infty}[h]_{\alpha}.

Moreover, we also have that

\left[\int_{0}^{L}f(\cdot,\xi,s)\ ds\right]_{\alpha}\leq\int_{0}^{L}\left[f(\cdot,\xi,s\right)]_{\alpha}\ ds.

Therefore, in order to bound the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm of (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) we have to estimate

\displaystyle[b_{13}(\cdot,\xi)]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\left[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}\frac{1}{1+\Theta(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\left[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}\right)\frac{1}{1+\Theta(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}\,ds

(5.27)

\displaystyle\quad:=b_{131}+b_{132},

\displaystyle[b_{14}(\cdot,\xi)]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\left[\frac{\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(\cdot,s)}-1\right)}{(1+\Theta(\cdot,s))^{2}}\nabla_{x}\Theta(\cdot,s)\right]_{\alpha}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-|\xi|s}\right)\left[\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)\frac{\nabla_{x}\Theta(\cdot,s)}{(1+\Theta(\cdot,s))^{2}}\right]_{\alpha}\,ds

(5.28)

\displaystyle\quad:=b_{141}+b_{142}.

Let us analyze the different
C^{\alpha}
semi-norms of the quantities involved in both terms. Let us start with the ones appearing in
b_{13}
, namely,
[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,s)]_{\alpha},[e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}]_{\alpha}
and
\left[\frac{1}{1+\Theta(x,s)}\right]_{\alpha}
.

The
[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,s)]_{\alpha}
bound

Using the fundamental theorem of calculus and Assumption
5.1
, we readily check that

\left|\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,s)\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x,\tau)\,d\tau\right|\leq s\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}.

which yields the an estimate for the
L^{\infty}
norm. Similarly, we find that

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(\cdot,s)\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{|\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x_{1},s)-\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x_{2},s)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{|\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x_{1},\tau)-\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{x}\Lambda(x_{2},\tau)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}\,d\tau\leq s\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}.

The
[e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}]_{\alpha}
bound

Trivially, the
L^{\infty}
estimate equals one. For the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm, we use again Assumption
5.1
, leading to

\displaystyle\left[e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}\right]_{\alpha}=\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{\left|e^{i\Lambda(x_{1},s)\cdot\xi}-e^{i\Lambda(x_{2},s)\cdot\xi}\right|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{1}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}\int_{\xi\cdot\Lambda(x_{1},s)}^{\xi\cdot\Lambda(x_{2},s)}\left|ie^{iw}\right|\,dw

\displaystyle\leq|\xi|\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{|\Lambda(x_{1},s)-\Lambda(x_{2},s)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq|\xi|\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{1}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{s}\left|\partial_{\tau}\left(\Lambda(x_{1},\tau)-\Lambda(x_{2},\tau)\right)\right|\,ds

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}.

The
[\frac{1}{1+\Theta(x,s)}]_{\alpha}
bound

Invoking Assumption
5.2
, the
L^{\infty}
bound follows immediately, namely,

\left|\dfrac{1}{1+\Theta(x,s)}\right|\leq\dfrac{1}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}.

The
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm can be estimated after finding an extra cancellation, this is

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{1+\Theta(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{1}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}\left|\frac{1}{1+\Theta(x_{1},s)}-\frac{1}{1+\Theta(x_{2},s)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{1}{\left(1+\Theta(x_{1})\right)\left(1+\Theta(x_{2})\right)}\frac{|\Theta(x_{1},s)-\Theta(x_{2},s)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}

(5.31)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\Theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}}{(1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty})^{2}}.

Therefore, combining estimates (
5.29
)-(
5.31
)
and using (
5.11
) we find that

\begin{split}b_{131}&\leq\sum_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{L}(s|\xi|)^{k_{\kappa}^{\gamma}}|\xi|^{-\gamma}e^{-s|\xi|}\left[\nabla_{x}\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}\frac{1}{1+\Theta(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}\,ds\\
&\leq\sum_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{L}(s|\xi|)^{k_{\kappa}^{\gamma}}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}e^{-s|\xi|}\frac{|\xi|s}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\left(\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}+s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}^{2}+\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2}}\frac{\|\Theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\right)\,ds\\
&\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\int_{0}^{|\xi|L}u^{1+k_{\kappa}^{\gamma}}(u+1)e^{-u}\,du\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\end{split}

where
C
can be taken independent of both
\xi
,
\Lambda
and
\Theta
. Note that we have used the smallness assumption on the norms of
\Lambda
and
\Theta
to absorb them by means of a universal constant.

In order to bound
b_{132}
in (
5.19
) we can repeat the previous computations just taking into account the extra summands that arise when expanding the term
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi e^{i\Lambda(\cdot,s)\cdot\xi}\right)
. Invoking Leibniz’s rule for for
k=1,2
we find that

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi_{k}e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\leq\gamma-\beta}{\gamma-\beta\choose\sigma}\left(\partial_{\xi}^{\sigma}\xi_{k}\right)\left(\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta-\sigma}e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt=\xi_{k}\left(i\Lambda(x,s)\right)^{\gamma-\beta}e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}+(\gamma_{k}-\beta_{k})\textbf{1}_{1\leq\gamma_{k}-\beta_{k}}\left(i\Lambda(x,s)\right)^{\gamma-\beta}(i\Lambda_{k}(x,s))^{-1}e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}.

Therefore, we can estimate its
L^{\infty}
norm and
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm rather easily by repeatedly using Assumption
5.1
to obtain the estimates

(5.32)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq Cs^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}_{C^{1}}\left(s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}+1\right),

(5.33)

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(\xi e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right)\right]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+s|\xi|)s^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}\left(s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}_{C^{2,\alpha}}+\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}_{C^{2,\alpha}}\right).

Hence recalling the expression of
b_{132}
defined in (
5.19
) we conclude that

\displaystyle b_{132}\leq C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\sum_{\kappa}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{L}s^{k^{\beta}_{\kappa}}|\xi|^{k_{\kappa}^{\beta}-|\beta|}s\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left(\frac{s^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}}{1+\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\left(s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}+1\right)\right.

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1+s|\xi|}{1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}}\left(|\xi|s^{|\gamma-\beta|}\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|}+s^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}\|\Lambda\|^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}\right)\right)\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|},

where we have used the fact that
|\gamma-\beta|\geq 1
, Assumption
5.1
and a change of variable
u=s|\xi|
to obtain an upper bound for the integral. Thus, bounds (
5.1
) and (
5.34
) yields

(5.35)

\|b_{13}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

To conclude the proof, we just to check that the same estimate holds for the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm of
b_{14}
given in (
5.28
). Noticing that since
\beta\neq\gamma
, we have that

\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}-1\right)=\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\Lambda(x,s)\cdot\xi}\right).

Hence, using Assumption
5.2
and (
5.11
) we can easily estimate
b_{142}
in (
5.28
) can be estimated by

(5.36)

b_{142}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

To bound
b_{141}
we have to deal with the term
e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1
. Using Assumption
5.1
we find that

\left|e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}e^{iw}\,dw\right|\leq|\xi||\Lambda(x,s)|\leq s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}},

and also the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm as

\left[e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(\cdot,s)}-1\right]_{\alpha}=\sup_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}}\frac{\left|e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x_{1},s)}-e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x_{2},s)}\right|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}=\left[e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}\leq s|\xi|\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}.

Recall that the later bound was already estimated in (
5.30
). Thus, similarly as for
b_{131}
we find that

(5.37)

b_{141}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

Thus, combining (
5.36
)-(
5.37
) we conclude that

(5.38)

\|b_{14}(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|}.

Therefore, we have shown that for any
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
multi-index

\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(b_{1}(\cdot,\xi)/|\xi|\right)\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq C\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|},

and hence
\frac{b_{1}}{|\xi|}\in S^{-1}(1+\alpha)
or similarly
b_{1}\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)
as desired.

In order to show that
\frac{b_{2}}{|\xi|}\in S^{1}(1+\alpha)
, where
b_{2}(x,\xi)
is defined in (
5.10
), we can mimic the same estimates derived for
b_{1}(x,\xi)
. Actually, the estimates are easier since we have to deal with the term
\Theta
instead of
e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1
which is simpler to handle. To avoid repetitive arguments, we directly provide the bound, namely,

\left\lVert\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(b_{2}(\cdot,\xi)/|\xi|\right)\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq C\|\Theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-1-|\gamma|},\forall\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{2}.

Thus
\frac{b_{2}}{|\xi|}\in S^{-1}(1+\alpha)
or similarly
b_{2}\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Notice that the regularity constants are optimal due to the threshold of regularity given by the functions
\Theta
. Moreover, if we examine again the estimates in the case of
\textsf{T}_{1}
the cancellation condition of
\Lambda
at
s=0
, as it might be expected, since we are deriving optimal estimates for pseudodifferential operators. This has led to bounds on the seminorms of the symbols depending on the
C^{1,\alpha}
norm of
\partial_{s}\Lambda
, which will be dominated in the sequel by the
C^{1,\alpha}
norm of the magnetic field via the definition of flow map.

Remark 5.5
.

Notice that by means of the previous computations, and due to the fact that the functions
\Lambda
and
\Theta
satisfy Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
respectively, we can estimate, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, the semi-norms of both symbols
b_{1},b_{2}
by

\|b_{1}\|_{0,1+\alpha,l},\|b_{2}\|_{0,1+\alpha,l}\leq C_{\alpha,l}\left(\|\Lambda\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}+\|\Theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\right).

A direct consequence of Proposition
5.3
is the following regularity result for the symbols
a_{1},a_{2}
defined in (
5.5
),(
5.6
) respectively:

Corollary 5.6
.

Under the same hypothesis of Proposition
5.3
, the symbols
a_{1},a_{2}
extend to symbols in
S^{0}(1+\alpha)
.

Proof.

Note that

a_{1}(x,\xi)=b_{1}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\quad a_{2}(x,\xi)=b_{2}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi).

Since
b_{1},b_{2}\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)
, it is sufficient to show that the remainder terms
\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi)\in S^{-\infty}(1+\alpha)
are smoothing symbols. Indeed, recalling that
(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}=\frac{|\xi|\sinh(|\xi|L)}{\cosh(|\xi|L)-1},
we have that

(5.39)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\left((\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}-|\xi|\right)\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\left(e^{-i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}-1\right)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds,

(5.40)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=\left((\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}-|\xi|\right)\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{\Theta(x,s)}{1+\Theta(x,s)}ds.

It easy to check that

(5.41)

|\left((\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}-|\xi|\right)|\leq Ce^{-|\xi|L},

and the integral terms can be estimated following the techniques developed in Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Next, we provide the regularity result for the smoothing symbols
a_{3},a_{4}
given in (
5.7
), (
5.8
) respectively.

Proposition 5.7
.

Suppose that Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
hold. Then, the symbols
a_{3},a_{4}
given in (
5.7
)-(
5.8
) respectively, extend to symbols in
S^{-\infty}(1+\alpha)
.

Proof.

Notice that the function
M(\xi,s)
defined in (
4.21
) is a smooth function
s
and decays exponentially in
\xi
. In particular, we have the simple bound

|M(\xi,s)|\leq Ce^{-|\xi|L}.

By the same reasoning as in Corollary
5.6
, the result follows.

∎

Remark 5.8
.

Notice that following the same estimates as the one derived in Proposition
5.3
for the symbols
b_{1},b_{2}
, we have that

(5.42)

\left\lVert a_{\kappa}\right\rVert_{0,1+\alpha,l}\leq C_{\alpha,l}\left(\left\lVert\Lambda\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}+\left\lVert\Theta\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}}\right),\ \kappa=1,\ldots,4.

We are ready to present the main result of this section, which shows the boundedness of the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa},\kappa=1,\ldots,4
, given in (
5.1
)-(
5.4
).

Theorem 5.9
.

Suppose that Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
hold. Then, the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa},\kappa=1,\ldots,4
, given in (
5.1
)-(
5.4
) respectively define bounded operators in
C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. More precisely, we have that

(5.43)

\|\textsf{T}_{\kappa}\|_{\mathcal{L}(C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\leq C(\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\|\Theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}),\quad\kappa=1,\ldots,4.

Proof of Theorem
5.9
.

In order to show the boundedness of the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa},\kappa=1,\ldots,4
, we first recall the results contained in Corollary
5.6
and Proposition
5.7
which demonstrate that

a_{\kappa}(x,\xi)\in S^{0}(1+\alpha)
for
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
. To conclude the proof we invoke Theorem
2.10
for
k=1
. Note that the precise estimate on the operator norm follows by the bounds on the symbols (
5.42
).

∎

5.2.
Estimates on the differences
\textsf{T}_{\kappa}

In this subsection, we will derive estimates for the difference operators. This will be needed in order to show the lower order
C^{\alpha}
contraction estimate in the general fixed point argument provided in Section
6
. The idea towards the proof follows the same lines as the one provided in the previous subsection, however some precise control of the constants involved in the computations is needed. To that purpose, for
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
and
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
, we denote the difference of the operators
\textsf{T}_{\kappa}
as

(5.44)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{1}^{d}=\textsf{T}_{1}[\Lambda_{1},\Theta_{1}]-\textsf{T}_{1}[\Lambda_{2},\Theta_{2}],\quad\textsf{T}_{3}^{d}=\textsf{T}_{3}[\Lambda_{1},\Theta_{1}]-\textsf{T}_{3}[\Lambda_{2},\Theta_{2}],

and

(5.45)

\displaystyle\textsf{T}_{2}^{d}=\textsf{T}_{2}[\Theta_{1}]-\textsf{T}_{2}[\Theta_{2}],\quad\textsf{T}_{4}^{d}=\textsf{T}_{4}[\Theta_{1}]-\textsf{T}_{4}[\Theta_{2}].

One big advantage of the pseudo-differential approach we are following is that
\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}
is once again the adjoint of a pseudo-differential operator,
\text{Op}(c_{\kappa})
. The symbol
c_{\kappa}
is, obviously, given by the difference

(5.46)

\displaystyle c_{1}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds

(5.47)

\displaystyle c_{2}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds,

(5.48)

\displaystyle c_{3}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds,

(5.49)

\displaystyle c_{4}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\xi))^{-1}\int_{0}^{L}M(\xi,s)e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds.

As in Subsection
5.1
, to simplify the exposition, we work with a more simplified version of
c_{1},c_{2}
, namely

(5.50)

\displaystyle d_{1}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds,

(5.51)

\displaystyle d_{2}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds.

Similarly as in the previous subsection, we have that

c_{1}(x,\xi)=d_{1}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{1}(x,\xi),\ c_{2}(x,\xi)=d_{2}(x,\xi)+\mathsf{r}_{2}(x,\xi),

where the the remainder terms are smoothing symbols. Therefore, it is sufficient to provide the regularity results for the symbols
d_{1}(x,\xi),d_{2}(x,\xi)
. Furthermore, the fast decay of the function
M(\xi,s)
, provides that the kernels defined out of
c_{3}(x,\xi),c_{4}(x,\xi)
are smooth along the same lines as in Proposition
5.7
.
One has to acknowledge that we want estimates in the case of less regularity, so we cannot directly use Theorem
2.10
to conclude that
\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{L}(C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
. However, if we prove adequate estimates for the symbols involved, we can get boundedness on the
C^{\alpha}
norm when
\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}
acts on
C^{1,\alpha}
functions.

Proposition 5.10
.

Suppose that Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
hold. Then, the symbols
d_{1},d_{2}
given in (
5.50
)-(
5.50
) respectively, extend to symbols in
S^{0}(\alpha)
. More precisely, we have that

(5.52)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(d_{1}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right),

(5.53)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(d_{2}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right).

Proof.

The proof follows closely the arguments of Proposition
5.3
. To avoid repetition, we provide the full details for the symbol
d_{1}
and just give the precise final estimate for the symbol
d_{2}
. We begin by writing them in a more convenient way, that explicitly shows the dependence in the distance between the functions
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
, and the functions
\Theta_{1}
and
\Theta_{2}
, namely,

\begin{split}d_{1}(x,\xi)&=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}-\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1}{1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds\\
&=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)ds\\
&+|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-1\right)\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{(\Theta_{1}(x,s)+1)(\Theta_{2}(x,s)+1)}ds=d_{11}(x,s)+d_{12}(x,s).\end{split}

Noticing that
|\xi|
is a Mikhlin-Hörmander multiplier of order
1
, we need to show that
d_{1}/|\xi|\in S^{-1}(\alpha)
.

L^{\infty}
bounds for
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(d_{1}(x,\xi)/|\xi|\right)

By applying Leibniz’s rule and recalling (
5.11
), we find that

\begin{split}\partial^{\gamma}_{\xi}\left(d_{11}/|\xi|\right)&=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\frac{\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}-e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}ds\\
&=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\frac{\left(\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{2}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{1}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}\right)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}ds\\
&=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-s|\xi|}s^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}}p^{\beta}_{\ell}(\xi)\frac{\left(\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{2}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{1}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}\right)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}ds.\end{split}

Here
p^{\beta}_{\kappa}(\xi)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\})
satisfying (
5.12
). By the fundamental theorem of calculus,

\begin{split}\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{2}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}&-\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{1}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}\\
&=\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\mathsf{h}_{\xi}(t\Lambda_{1}(x,s)+(1-t)\Lambda_{2}(x,s))dt,\end{split}

where
\mathsf{h}_{\xi}(r)=r^{\gamma-\beta}e^{i\xi\cdot r}
. Note that

\nabla\mathsf{h}(r)=\mathscr{F}(r)e^{i\xi\cdot r}+r^{\gamma-\beta}\xi e^{i\xi\cdot r},

with
\mathscr{F}(r)
a vector where every element consists on a monomial of degree
|\gamma-\beta|-1
. Furthermore, since by Assumption
5.1

\Lambda_{1}(x,0)=\Lambda_{2}(x,0)=0
, and
\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{C^{1}},\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{2}}\leq 1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{2}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-\Lambda^{\gamma-\beta}_{1}(x,s)e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{1}(x,s)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(s^{|\gamma-\beta|-1}+|\xi|s^{|\gamma-\beta|}\right)|\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)|

(5.54)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+s|\xi|\right)s^{|\gamma-\beta|}\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}.

Hence, combing (
5.54
) and the fact that
\|\Theta_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq 1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(d_{11}/|\xi|\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}\frac{C}{|\xi|^{|\gamma|}}\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}\left(|\xi|s\right)^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}+|\gamma-\beta|}\left(1+s|\xi|\right)e^{-s|\xi|}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}\frac{1}{|\xi|^{|\gamma|+1}}\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}+|\gamma+\beta|}(1+u)e^{-u}du

(5.55)

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}.

Let us proceed to derive the
L^{\infty}
bounds for
d_{12}
. Similarly, using Leibniz rule we have that

\begin{split}\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)&=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1\right)\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{\left(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)\right)\left(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)\right)}ds\\
&=\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}{\gamma\choose\beta}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}s^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}}p^{\beta}_{\ell}(\xi)\left(i\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)^{\gamma-\beta}e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda(x,s)}\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{\left(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)\right)\left(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)\right)}ds\\
&+\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}\int^{L}_{0}s^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}}p^{\beta}_{\ell}(\xi)\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1\right)\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s))(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s))}ds.\end{split}

Using that
\Lambda_{2}(x,0)=0
by Assumption
5.1
, we derive the bounds

\left|\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right|^{|\gamma-\beta|}\leq s^{|\gamma-\beta|}\|\Lambda\|_{C^{1}}^{|\gamma-\beta|},\quad\left|e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1\right|\leq s|\xi|\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\leq\gamma\\
\beta\neq\gamma\end{subarray}}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}(|\xi|s)^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}+|\gamma-\beta|}e^{-s|\xi|}ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}(s|\xi|)^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}}e^{-s|\xi|}ds

(5.56)

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}.

Combing (
5.55
) and (
5.56
), we obtain the
L^{\infty}
bound

(5.57)

\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{1}(x,\xi)}{|\xi|}\right)\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\left(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}\right).

C^{\alpha}
bounds for
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(d_{1}(x,\xi)/|\xi|\right)

Next, we derive the
C^{\alpha}
bounds. We begin with the estimates for
d_{11}
. It is more convenient to rewrite
d_{11}
as follows

d_{11}(x,\xi)=|\xi|\int_{0}^{L}e^{-|\xi|s}\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)}-1\right)\ ds.

Using Leibniz rule, we find that

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{11}}{|\xi|}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\left(\frac{e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)}-1\right)\right)ds

(5.58)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\delta\leq\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}{\beta\choose\delta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\frac{\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}\partial^{\beta-\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)}-1\right)ds.

Let us of compute the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm of the different terms involved in (
5.58
).

Bounds for
[\frac{1}{1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)}]_{\alpha}

The
L^{\infty}
norm can be dominated by
\frac{1}{1-\|\Theta_{1}\|_{\infty}}
. On the other hand, the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm can be bounded by

(5.59)

\left[\frac{1}{1+\Theta_{1}(\cdot,s)}\right]_{\alpha}=\sum_{x\neq y}\frac{|\Theta_{1}(y,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)|}{|1+\Theta_{1}(x,s)||1+\Theta_{2}(x,s)|}\leq\frac{[\Theta_{1}]_{\alpha}}{(1-\|\Theta_{1}\|_{\infty})^{2}}.

Bounds for
[\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)]_{\alpha}

We readily check that

\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)=\left(i\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)^{\delta}e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}
.
Moreover, due to Assumption
5.1
we use that
\Lambda_{2}(x,0)=0
to find that

\left|\left(i\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)^{\delta}e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right|\leq s^{|\delta|}\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1}}^{\delta}.

For the
C^{\alpha}
semi-norm, we realize that
[\cdot]_{\alpha}\leq C\|\cdot\|_{C^{1}}
. Since
\Lambda_{2}\in C^{1}
, we obtain

\nabla_{x}\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}\right)=\nabla_{x}\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\cdot\left[\mathscr{F}(\Lambda_{2}(x,s))+\xi\left(i\Lambda(x,s)\right)^{\delta}\right]e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)},

where
\mathscr{F}
is a vector where each component is a monomials of degree
|\delta|-1
. Thus, since
\nabla_{x}\Lambda_{2}(x,0)=0
, and
\left\lVert\Lambda_{2}\right\rVert_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
we conclude that

(5.60)

\left[\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(\cdot,s)}\right)\right]_{\alpha}\leq\left\|\partial^{\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(\cdot,s)}\right)\right\|_{C^{1}}\leq Cs^{|\delta|}\left(1+s|\xi|\right).

Bounds for
[\partial^{\beta-\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)}-1\right)]_{\alpha}

The
L^{\infty}
and the
C^{\alpha}
bounds are obtained similarly as in the proof of Proposition
5.3
, just by substituting
\Lambda
by
\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}
. Then, we conclude that

(5.61)

\left\|\partial^{\beta-\delta}_{\xi}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\left(\Lambda_{1}(x,s)-\Lambda_{2}(x,s)\right)}-1\right)\right\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\leq C\left(|\xi|s\right)^{|\beta-\delta|}|\xi|^{-|\beta-\delta|}\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}.

Hence, gathering bounds (
5.59
)-(
5.61
) and using the Hölder semi-norm estimate (
5.26
) we infer that

(5.62)

\left[\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{11}}{|\xi|}\right)\right]_{\alpha}\leq C\sum_{\delta\leq\beta\leq\gamma}\sum_{\ell}\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}|\xi|^{-|\beta|}\int_{0}^{L}s^{k^{\gamma-\beta}_{\ell}}p^{\gamma-\beta}_{\ell}(\xi)\left(s|\xi|\right)^{|\delta|}(1+s|\xi|)\left(|\xi|s\right)^{|\beta-\delta|}|ds.

Since
|p_{\ell}|\lesssim|\xi|^{k_{\ell}}
, we obtain that

(5.63)

\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{11}}{|\xi|}\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}},

and therefore,
d_{11}\in S^{0}(\alpha)
.

To conclude that
d_{1}\in S^{0}(\alpha)
, we have to perform similar estimates for
d_{12}
. Indeed, we first compute the
\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)
, namely

\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)=\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}{\gamma\choose\beta}\int_{0}^{L}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\left(e^{-s|\xi|}\right)\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma-\beta}\left(e^{i\xi\cdot\Lambda_{2}(x,s)}-1\right)\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s))(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s))}ds.

The
L^{\infty}
estimate is straightforward. Using the fact that
\Lambda_{2}(x,0)=0
and formula (
5.16
) to expand
e^{-s|\xi|}
we infer that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\sum_{\beta\leq\gamma}\sum_{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}e^{-s|\xi|}\left(s|\xi|\right)^{k^{\beta}_{\ell}}\left(s|\xi|\right)^{|\gamma-\beta|}ds

(5.64)

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{\infty}.

For the
C^{\alpha}
, we just notice that

\left\|\frac{\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)}{(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s))(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s))}\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq\|\Theta_{2}(x,s)-\Theta_{1}(x,s)\|_{C^{\alpha}}\left\|\frac{1}{(1+\Theta_{1}(x,s))(1+\Theta_{2}(x,s))}\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}.

The right factor above can be bounded easily by using Assumption
5.2
and estimate

\left[\frac{1}{1+\Theta}\right]_{\alpha}\leq\frac{\|\Theta\|_{C^{\alpha}}}{\left(1-\|\Theta\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}}.

Therefore, we have obtained that

(5.65)

\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{12}}{|\xi|}\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}}.

Combining (
5.63
) and (
5.65
) we finally conclude that

\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{1}}{|\xi|}\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\left(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right),

showing that
d_{1}/|\xi|\in S^{-1}(\alpha)
and hence
d_{1}\in S^{0}(\alpha)
.

It is not difficult to check that we can mimic the same arguments derived to provide the final bound for
d_{1}
for the symbol
d_{2}
. More precisely, one can derive the estimate

\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(\frac{d_{2}}{|\xi|}\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|-1}\left(\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right),

which shows that
d_{2}/|\xi|\in S^{-1}(\alpha)
and hence
d_{2}\in S^{0}(\alpha)
.
∎
Using the same ideas as in Corollary
5.6
, we can derive its counterpart for the difference operators.

Corollary 5.11
.

Under the same hypothesis of Proposition
5.10
, the symbols
c_{1},c_{2}
extend to symbols in
S^{0}(\alpha)
. More precisely, we have that

(5.66)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(c_{1}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right),

(5.67)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(c_{2}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right).

Next, we provide in the same spirit as in Proposition
5.7
, the regularity result for the smoothing symbols
c_{3},c_{4}
given in (
5.48
), (
5.49
) respectively that reads

Proposition 5.12
.

Let Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
hold. Then, the symbols
c_{3},c_{4}
given in (
5.48
)-(
5.49
) respectively, extend to symbols in
S^{-\infty}(\alpha)
. In particular, we have that

(5.68)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(c_{3}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right),

(5.69)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\xi}^{\gamma}\left(c_{4}(\cdot,\xi)\right)\right\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|^{-|\gamma|}\left(\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}} \right).

The precise statement of the main result for the difference of the operators
\mathsf{T}_{\kappa}
is the following

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
satisfy Assumption
5.1
and
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
satisfy Assumption
5.2
. Then, for
j_{0}^{\ell}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
\ell=1,2,
the operators
\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}
,
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
given in (
5.44
)-(
5.45
) satisfy

(5.70)

\displaystyle\|\textsf{T}^{d}_{1}j_{0}^{\ell}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}+\|\textsf{T}^{d}_{3}j^{\ell}_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega)}+\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\Omega)}),

(5.71)

\displaystyle\|\textsf{T}^{d}_{2}j_{0}^{\ell}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}+\|\textsf{T}^{d}_{4}j^{\ell}_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\Omega)}),

Proof of Theorem
5.13
.

Due to Proposition
5.10
, Corollary
5.11

and Proposition
5.12
we infer that the symbols
c_{\kappa}(x,\xi)\in S^{0}(\alpha)
, for
\kappa=1,\ldots,4
. Therefore,

\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}|_{C^{1,\alpha}}
is the restriction of a pseudo-differential operator given by a symbol in
S^{0}(\alpha)
. Therefore,
\textsf{T}^{d}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{L}(C^{\alpha})
and, in particular, due to the estimates (
5.66
)- (
5.69
) we have that the bounds (
5.70
) and (
5.71
) hold.
∎

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt}_{2}^{+}(\xi)\left(\frac{mn}{|\xi|^{2}}\chi_{\{\xi\neq 0\}}\right)\frac{|\xi|}{\sinh(|\xi|L)}e^{ir\cdot\xi}.

Furthermore, the functions
h^{\pm}_{\ell}
(
\ell=1,2
) are defined as

(5.74)

\begin{split}h_{2}^{-}(x,y)&=\int_{0}^{x}f(s,y,0)\,ds-\frac{x}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(s,y,0)\,ds,\\
h_{1}^{-}(x,y)&=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{y}f(s,t,0)\,dtds+\frac{y}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(s,t,0)\,dtds,\\
h_{2}^{+}(x,y)&=\int_{0}^{x}f(s,y,L)\,ds-\frac{x}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(s,y,L)\,ds,\\
h_{1}^{+}(x,y)&=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{y}f(s,t,L)\,dtds+\frac{y}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(s,t,L)\,dtds.\end{split}

We need to give a precise sense of the different operators listed above. In the following Lemma we derive common estimates for the different
\mathcal{Z}_{\ell}
in terms of the
C^{2,\alpha}
norm of the boundary value
f
, in spite of the different level of regularity of the
h_{\ell}^{\pm}
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
f\in C^{2,\alpha}(\partial\Omega)
. Then,
\mathcal{Z}_{1}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(5.75)

\|\mathcal{Z}_{\ell}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}},\quad\ell=1,2,

holds.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

We have to understand the functions
\mathcal{Z}_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,2,
as periodic distributions with Fourier coefficients given by those in expressions (
5.72
) and (
5.73
). The operators defined by
f\mapsto\mathcal{Z}_{\ell}
are Fourier multipliers,
\ell=1,2
. Since both functions are the same, we will just focus on
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
. This can clearly be written as the sum of four summands that we denote
\mathcal{Z}_{11}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{12}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{13}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{14}
.
The multiplier in
\mathcal{Z}_{11}
is given by

(5.76)

\left(\frac{mn}{|\xi|^{2}}\chi_{\{\xi\neq 0\}}\right)\frac{|\xi|\cosh(|\xi|L)}{\sinh(|\xi|L)},

that corresponds with the consecutive application of the multipliers

\frac{m}{|\xi|}
and
\frac{n}{|\xi|}.

Notice that both are zero order Mikhlin-Hörmander multipliers. On the other hand, we have that

\frac{|\xi|\cosh(|\xi|L)}{\sinh(|\xi|L)}=|\xi|+\mathsf{r}(\xi),

this is Mikhlin-Hörmander multiplier
|\xi|
plus a smoothing multiplier
\mathsf{r}(\xi)
, similarly as in (
5.41
). Combining the previous ideas, we conclude that (
5.76
) equals is Mikhlin-Hörmander multiplier of order 1 plus a smoothing operator. Theferore, taking into account that
h_{1}^{-}\in C^{3,\alpha}
we find that

\left\|\mathcal{Z}_{11}\right\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C\|h_{1}^{-}\|_{C^{3,\alpha}}\leq C\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}.

The second and fourth term in
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
are straightforward since the involved multiplier

\frac{|\xi|}{\sinh(|\xi|L)}
decays exponentially in
\xi
and hence
\mathcal{Z}_{12},\mathcal{Z}_{14}
are
smoothing operator. Moreover, we have the estimates

\|\mathcal{Z}_{12}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}},\,\|\mathcal{Z}_{14}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}.

The remaining term
\mathcal{Z}_{13}
is more subtle, because
h_{2}^{-}\notin C^{3,\alpha}
. Therefore, to obtain the desired estimate, we need to modify the previous argument. We notice that although
h_{2}^{-}\notin C^{3,\alpha}
in both variables, it has more differentiability in the
x
-variable. Thus, we have rearrange the multipliers in the definition of
\mathcal{Z}_{12}
to obtain the correct estimate. More precisely, notice that

\partial_{1}h^{-}_{2}=f(x,y,0)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(s,y,0)\ ds,

which shows that

\|\partial_{1}h_{2}^{-}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}}.

Furthermore, recalling that
|\xi|^{2}=n^{2}+m^{2}
we infer that

(5.77)

\begin{split}\hbox{\hskip
